### Research methods

#### Experimental design and variables

*Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams) · Prerequisites: Scientific methodologies and variables (Psychology, Units 1 & 2); Sampling and experimental designs (Psychology, Units 1 & 2)*

**Q1232.** In an experiment, the independent variable is
<!-- id: psych34a3-ed-1 -->
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A*

- A. the group that receives no treatment
- B. the variable measured to determine the effect of the manipulation
- C. the variable that the researcher manipulates or that differs between conditions
- D. any variable that is not controlled

**Q1233.** A researcher tests whether caffeine improves reaction time. Participants in Group A are given a caffeinated drink and those in Group B are given a decaffeinated drink that looks identical. Group B is the
<!-- id: psych34a3-ed-2 -->
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A*

- A. control group
- B. confounding variable
- C. placebo-free group
- D. experimental group

**Q1234.** A study uses the same participants in both the experimental and control conditions. This is a
<!-- id: psych34a3-ed-3 -->
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A*

- A. quasi-experimental design with no IV
- B. between-subjects design
- C. repeated measures design
- D. matched participants design

**Q1235.** A researcher wants to reduce the effect of order in a repeated measures design. Which strategy is most appropriate?
<!-- id: psych34a3-ed-4 -->
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A*

- A. Counterbalancing the order of conditions
- B. Using a larger sample only
- C. Using a single-blind procedure
- D. Removing the control group

**Q1236.** Which of the following describes a confounding variable?
<!-- id: psych34a3-ed-5 -->
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A*

- A. A variable that is measured as an outcome
- B. A random error in measurement
- C. A variable that the researcher intentionally manipulates
- D. An extraneous variable that systematically varies with the IV and may affect the DV, making it hard to determine the cause of the effect

**Q1237.** A researcher investigates whether listening to music during study improves exam scores. Identify the independent variable, the dependent variable and two variables that should be controlled. (4 marks)
<!-- id: psych34a3-ed-6 -->
*Info: Investigation design · about 4 marks · about 5 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); short answer, as in Section B*

*Written answer*

**Q1238.** Explain the purpose of random allocation and a placebo in an experiment. (3 marks)
<!-- id: psych34a3-ed-7 -->
*Info: Investigation design · about 3 marks · about 4 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); short answer, as in Section B*

*Written answer*

**Q1239.** A researcher compares sleep-deprived participants with participants who slept normally on a memory task. Group allocation is based on whether the participant stayed up the night before by choice. Explain why this is a limitation and how it could be improved. (3 marks)
<!-- id: psych34a3-ed-8 -->
*Info: Investigation design · about 3 marks · about 4 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); short answer, as in Section B*

*Written answer*

**Q1240.** In a study of caffeine and reaction time, the caffeine group happened to include mostly experienced gamers. Gaming experience is best described as:
<!-- id: vce-psych34-ed-c1 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A; harder than most exam questions*

- A. The independent variable, because it was different between the two groups
- B. A controlled variable, because it was kept the same within each group
- C. The dependent variable, because reaction time was measured in the gamers
- D. A confounding variable, because it differs systematically between groups and may affect the DV

#### Data, statistics and conclusions

*Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams) · Prerequisites: Experimental design and variables; Data analysis and conclusions (Psychology, Units 1 & 2)*

<!-- id: psych34a3-ds-1 -->

- A. A transcript of an interview
- B. A participant's description of how tired they feel in their own words
- C. The number of words recalled from a list
- D. A researcher's observation notes about a participant's mood

- A. There are a few unusually high scores
- B. The mode is 12
- C. The data are symmetrical
- D. The data are negatively skewed

<!-- id: psych34a3-ds-3 -->
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A*

- A. the results are not statistically significant
- B. the results are statistically significant, so the null hypothesis can be rejected at the 0.05 level, and there is a 3% probability that results at least this extreme occurred by chance alone if there were no effect
- C. the hypothesis has a 97% chance of being true
- D. the effect is large and important

- A. Screen use causes sleep loss
- B. There is a strong negative association: more screen use is associated with less sleep
- C. There is a weak positive association
- D. There is no association

- A. the use of quantitative data
- B. the use of random allocation
- C. the small sample size limiting the generalisability of the results

**Q1249.** Hours of sleep lost by five participants were 2, 3, 3, 4 and 18. The best measure of central tendency to report is the:
<!-- id: vce-psych34-ds-c1 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Data analysis · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A; harder than most exam questions*

- A. Mean (6), because it uses all the data
- B. Median (3), because the outlier of 18 distorts the mean

#### Ethics and validity

*Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams) · Prerequisites: Experimental design and variables; Ethics in psychological research (Psychology, Units 1 & 2)*

**Q1250.** Which ethical principle requires that participants are told they can leave a study at any time without penalty?
<!-- id: psych34a3-ev-1 -->
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A*

- A. Right to withdraw
- B. Debriefing
- C. Beneficence
- D. Confidentiality

**Q1251.** A researcher uses deception in a study about conformity. Which action is essential after the study finishes?
<!-- id: psych34a3-ev-2 -->
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A*

- A. Preventing participants from withdrawing their data
- B. Destroying the data
- C. Debriefing the participants and explaining the true purpose
- D. Offering payment only to those who complete the study

**Q1252.** Which of the following best describes internal validity?
<!-- id: psych34a3-ev-3 -->

- A. The extent to which the experiment measures what it claims to measure and the results are due to the IV, not other factors
- B. The consistency of results over repeated trials
- C. The extent to which the sample is large
- D. The extent to which the results can be generalised to the population

- B. External validity
- C. Precision
- D. Confidentiality

### Challenge questions

#### Challenge set

**Q1259.** Explain how a specific phobia of dogs could be precipitated by classical conditioning and perpetuated by operant conditioning.

**Q1260.** A double-blind procedure is used mainly to control:
<!-- id: vce-psych34-challenge-2 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A; harder than most exam questions · Topic: Experimental design and variables*

- A. The placebo effect only
- B. Both the placebo effect and the experimenter effect
- C. Order effects
- D. Participants' individual differences

**Q1261.** In a within-subjects study, participants complete a memory test after a full night's sleep and again after partial sleep deprivation. Counterbalancing is used to:
<!-- id: vce-psych34-challenge-3 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Investigation design · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A; harder than most exam questions · Topic: Experimental design and variables*

- A. Control order effects such as practice and fatigue
- B. Remove participant differences
- C. Make the sample more representative
- D. Ensure informed consent

**Q1262.** A patient with amygdala damage (but intact hippocampi) hears a tone paired with a loud horn. Afterwards the patient can say the tone predicts the horn, but shows no fear response to the tone. This suggests:
<!-- id: vce-psych34-challenge-4 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Application · 1 mark · about 1.5 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A; harder than most exam questions · Topic: Brain regions in memory*

- A. The hippocampus is needed for conditioned fear, while the amygdala supports explicit memory of the pairing
- B. The cerebellum stores the conditioned fear response, so the amygdala is not involved at all
- C. The amygdala is needed for conditioned fear, while the hippocampus supports explicit memory of the pairing
- D. Fear conditioning is a form of explicit memory, so it depends on the hippocampus alone

<!-- id: vce-psych34-challenge-5 -->

**Q1264.** Dopamine is described as a neuromodulator rather than only a neurotransmitter because it:
<!-- id: vce-psych34-challenge-6 -->

*Info: Conceptual · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A; harder than most exam questions · Topic: Neurotransmitters and neuromodulators*

- A. Can affect many neurons at once and alter their responsiveness over a longer period
- B. Is always inhibitory, so it reduces the firing of every neuron that it reaches
- C. Only acts on one post-synaptic neuron, so its effects are very precise
- D. Is produced only in the gut, so it reaches the brain through the blood

**Q1265.** Researchers recruit 40 volunteers from their own university's gym to study exercise and stress. The main limitation of this sample is that:
<!-- id: vce-psych34-challenge-7 -->

- A. It is a convenience sample that may not represent the population of interest
- B. It is too large, so differences between participants are hidden by the average
- C. It removes all extraneous variables, so the results cannot be generalised
- D. It is a stratified sample, so every subgroup of the population is over-represented

**Q1268.** Shift workers often have poorer sleep. Which recommendation best uses zeitgebers to help?
<!-- id: vce-psych34-challenge-10 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Evaluation · 1 mark · about 1 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multiple choice, as in Section A; harder than most exam questions · Topic: Sleep hygiene and zeitgebers*

- A. Bright light just before sleeping during the day, to signal to the SCN that sleep should begin
- B. Eating large meals just before sleep, so that digestion helps the body relax and fall asleep
- C. Changing sleep times every day, so the body does not settle into any one fixed rhythm
- D. Bright light during the night shift and dark, blackout conditions when sleeping during the day

### Exam-style questions (mixed topics)

**Q1269.** Exam stress and a breathing study
<!-- id: vce-psych34-x1 -->
*Info: Conceptual, Investigation design, Application, Recall · 11 marks · about 14 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multi-part question, as in Section B · Topics: Divisions of the nervous system, Transactional model of stress and coping, Stress responses and the GAS, Coping strategies and flexibility, Experimental design and variables*
*Multi-part question · 11 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Psychology exam, Section B*

Mia has her first VCE exam in two weeks. Whenever she thinks about it, her heart races and her palms sweat.

**a.** Identify the division of the nervous system responsible for Mia's racing heart, and explain its role in this response. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Using Lazarus and Folkman's transactional model of stress and coping, describe the primary appraisal Mia is making. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Mia's stress continues for several weeks. Using Selye's General Adaptation Syndrome, explain why she is more likely to catch a cold during this time. *(2 marks)*

**d.** Give one approach strategy Mia could use to cope with her exam stress. *(1 mark)*

A researcher tested whether 10 minutes of slow breathing before a test reduces anxiety. Sixty Year 12 students were randomly allocated to a group that did slow breathing or a group that read quietly for 10 minutes. Each student then rated their anxiety on a scale from 1 to 10.

**e.** Identify the independent variable in this experiment. *(1 mark)*

**f.** Name the experimental design used. *(1 mark)*

**g.** Identify one extraneous variable, and explain how random allocation helps to control it. *(2 marks)*

**Q1270.** Late nights, memory and a nervous bell
<!-- id: vce-psych34-x2 -->
*Info: Conceptual, Application, Recall · 11 marks · about 14 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multi-part question, as in Section B · Topics: Atkinson-Shiffrin multi-store model, Brain regions in memory, Sleep deprivation, Sleep hygiene and zeitgebers, Classical conditioning*
*Multi-part question · 11 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Psychology exam, Section B*

Liam is studying for his exams. After a week of going to bed at about 2 am and getting up at 7 am for school, he is struggling to remember what he studied.

**a.** According to the Atkinson–Shiffrin multi-store model, explain what happens to information on Liam's notes once it reaches his sensory memory. *(2 marks)*

**b.** State the capacity and duration of short-term memory. *(1 mark)*

**c.** Explain the role of the hippocampus in forming Liam's explicit memories of what he studied. *(2 marks)*

**d.** Liam has built up a sleep debt. Describe two likely effects of partial sleep deprivation on his functioning. *(2 marks)*

**e.** Liam uses his phone in bed until 2 am. Explain how this could delay the time he feels sleepy, referring to melatonin. *(2 marks)*

At school, a bell rings at the start of every exam. The bell used to mean nothing to Liam, but now he feels nervous whenever he hears it, even outside exam time.

**f.** Using the three-phase model of classical conditioning, identify the neutral stimulus and the conditioned response in this example. *(2 marks)*

**Q1271.** A fear of dogs
<!-- id: vce-psych34-x3 -->
*Info: Conceptual, Application · 10 marks · about 13 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multi-part question, as in Section B · Topics: Specific phobia: contributing factors, Classical conditioning, Operant conditioning, Neurotransmitters and neuromodulators, Specific phobia: evidence-based interventions*
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Psychology exam, Section B*

Priya, 17, has been intensely afraid of dogs since she was bitten at age 6. She now avoids parks and will not visit friends who own dogs. When she sees a dog, her heart races and she feels she must escape.

**a.** Explain why Priya's fear is likely to be a specific phobia rather than an ordinary fear. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Use classical conditioning to explain how the bite could have led to Priya's fear of dogs. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Use operant conditioning to explain how Priya's avoidance keeps the phobia going. *(2 marks)*

**d.** Explain how GABA dysfunction could contribute to specific phobia. *(2 marks)*

**e.** Describe how systematic desensitisation could be used to treat Priya's phobia. *(2 marks)*

**Q1272.** Night shifts and sleep
<!-- id: vce-psych34-x4 -->
*Info: Conceptual, Recall, Investigation design · 10 marks · about 13 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multi-part question, as in Section B · Topics: Circadian rhythm sleep disorders and treatments, Sleep deprivation, NREM and REM sleep, Experimental design and variables*
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Psychology exam, Section B*

Sam is a nurse who works a run of night shifts, sleeping during the day.

**a.** Explain how working night shifts disrupts Sam's circadian rhythm. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Sam has been awake for 17 hours by the end of a shift. Compare his likely performance with a blood alcohol concentration (BAC). *(2 marks)*

**c.** Describe how bright light therapy could help Sam adjust to night shifts. *(2 marks)*

**d.** Which type of sleep dominates the first half of a typical night, and which dominates the second half? *(2 marks)*

A researcher measured the reaction times of 12 nurses after a night shift and again after a day shift.

**e.** Name the experimental design and give one advantage of using it in this study. *(2 marks)*

**Q1273.** Challenge: exam stress and sleep
<!-- id: vce-psych34-cx1 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Conceptual, Recall, Application · 10 marks · about 13 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multi-part question, as in Section B; harder than most exam questions · Topics: Transactional model of stress and coping, Stress responses and the GAS, Sleep hygiene and zeitgebers, Sleep deprivation, Coping strategies and flexibility*
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original challenge question in the style of the VCE Psychology exam, Section B*

Ava is a Year 12 student. In the month before her exams she thinks, 'If I fail, I won't get into my course.' She then decides she has no time to ask her teachers for help. She studies on her laptop until 1 am most nights and has to wake at 6:30 am.

**a.** Identify Ava's primary and secondary appraisals using the transactional model of stress and coping. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Explain how cortisol released over several weeks could affect Ava. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Explain how screen use until 1 am is likely to affect when Ava falls asleep. *(2 marks)*

**d.** Ava gets about 5.5 hours of sleep a night. Describe one likely affective and one likely cognitive effect of this partial sleep deprivation. *(2 marks)*

**e.** Recommend one approach coping strategy for Ava and explain why it would suit her situation better than avoidance. *(2 marks)*

**Q1274.** Challenge: testing a phobia treatment
<!-- id: vce-psych34-cx2 -->
*Challenge question*
*Info: Investigation design, Calculation, Conceptual · 10 marks · about 13 min · Exam relevance: High (comes up in most exams); multi-part question, as in Section B; harder than most exam questions · Topics: Experimental design and variables, Data, statistics and conclusions, Specific phobia: evidence-based interventions, Ethics and validity*
*Multi-part question · 10 marks · Original challenge question in the style of the VCE Psychology exam, Section B*

Researchers recruited 30 adults with a specific phobia of spiders. Participants were randomly allocated to two groups. Group A received six sessions of systematic desensitisation; Group B received six sessions of psychoeducation only. Each participant rated their anxiety (0 = none, 10 = extreme) when shown a live spider in a sealed tank before and after treatment.

| Group | Mean anxiety before | Mean anxiety after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A: systematic desensitisation | 8.2 | 3.1 |
| B: psychoeducation only | 8.0 | 6.5 |

**a.** Write a hypothesis for this study. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Identify the experimental design and give one advantage of it for this study. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Calculate the change in mean anxiety for each group and state a conclusion. *(2 marks)*

**d.** Explain how systematic desensitisation reduces the fear response. *(2 marks)*

**e.** Identify one ethical guideline the researchers must follow, and explain why it matters in this study. *(2 marks)*
